````
id: art_7GxreYjATkC5
type: experiment
title: Peer agreement vs paid AI judges on held-out logic
summary: >-
  T1 head-on test on held-out NL->FOL dataset E (8,507 real candidates, 700 sentences). The frozen peer-agreement consensus
  metric (c_score_align, exp 5) and PEER+TEXT fusion (p_peer_text) are compared with newly scored paid API comparators: gemini-2.5-flash-lite
  rubric-A JSON judge (disguised + original, every parseable row), gpt-4.1-nano P(YES) judge (both views), gemini-3.1-pro-preview
  frontier judge (original view, 284-row frame; disguised view blocked by the pre-registered shared-key reserve rule), API
  round trip (flash-lite verbaliser -> DeBERTa NLI/mpnet) and API SC-5 (nano, T=0.7, z3 modulo vocabulary); plus S4_full,
  a cross-fitted stack of all 28 baselines. Pre-registered in prereg_T1.json (sha256 c2a6cf84, frozen before any sweep score).
  Main population R_AB E_POOL PRIMARY n=2,686 (1,822 ERROR/864 CORRECT, 292 sentences); sentence-cluster bootstrap B=2000.
  RESULTS: stratified AUROC c_score_align 0.741 vs flash-lite disguised 0.642 (pooled 0.782 vs 0.696). (a) CONFIRMED: strat
  dAUROC +0.099 [0.049,0.146]; long pool +0.116 [0.070,0.163]; R_A L20+EXC +0.299; also vs best cheap judge (nano orig) +0.089
  [0.043,0.134]. (b) CONFIRMED: nested [S4_full+c]-S4_full strat +0.039 [0.021,0.057], permutation-null p95 0.009; c alone
  ~= S4_full (+0.006 [-0.031,0.042]). (d) CONFIRMED: frame ratio vs frontier 0.957 [0.887,1.034], consensus $1.2e-4 vs frontier
  $3.65e-3/item, nested frame gain +0.037 [0.008,0.066]. VEX aligner-confound control CONFIRMED: +0.306 [0.232,0.376]. M3
  (smaller length slope than the judge) DISCONFIRMED: +0.146 [-0.051,0.361]. Overall T1 (this artifact): CONFIRMED. Caveats:
  no advantage on the short FOLIO CTRL stratum (-0.069 [-0.23,0.10]) or on <12-word / <=1-condition sentences; the advantage
  concentrates in long, conditioned, exception sentences (EXC +0.197). Consensus misses aligner-masked MEANING_RENAME errors
  (recall 0.07 at FA 0.10 vs judges 0.13-0.20) and peer-endorsed errors (0.00). Flash-lite contamination DiD +0.105 [0.006,0.201]
  (MDE 0.14). Test-retest Spearman 0.975. API spend $2.395 (cap $4.60). Independent checks: tests/audit_T1.py (5 headline
  numbers match to 1e-3) and tests/audit_raw_T1.py (raw files -> identical confirmatory/long-pool delta and frame ratio; placebos
  fail as required). Files: results/analysis_T1.json, verdict_T1.json, tables_T1.md, per_item_T1.jsonl, deviations.json, api_cost_ledger.json,
  llm_cache.jsonl; figures/forest_T1, complexity_curves; full_method_out.json (predict_* oriented scores for every metric).

GITHUB_DATA_URL = "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ai-inventor-papers/ai-invention-eff060-layered-gold-free-checks-for-logic/fork/run_An1uy2kF2SjZ/round-3/experiment-6/demo/mini_demo_data.json"

from pathlib import Path

def load_data():
    try:
        import urllib.request
        with urllib.request.urlopen(GITHUB_DATA_URL) as response:
            return json.loads(response.read().decode())
    except Exception: pass
    local = Path("mini_demo_data.json")
    if local.exists(): return json.loads(local.read_text())
    raise FileNotFoundError("Could not load mini_demo_data.json")
```
````

### [3] HUMAN-USER prompt · 2026-09-25 16:21:49 UTC

```
I work on converting natural-language text into first-order logic (NL→FOL, autoformalization) with
LLM pipelines. Evaluation is the bottleneck. Correct FOL for a sentence is not unique — different
predicate names, different decompositions of the same concept, and logically equivalent rewrites can
all be correct. Gold FOL annotations are rare and expensive. The metrics in use either need a gold
formula (exact match, BLEU, prover-checked equivalence to the gold) or only check that the output
parses. In my own pilot I used cheap structural metrics: whether a set of generated formulas loads
together without conflicts, whether predicate arities stay consistent, how many predicates are used
but never defined, and how similar the predicate sets are across reruns. Those measure stability and
composability, not whether a formula says what the sentence says.

Find new metrics for NL→FOL output quality. Operating situation: you are given a natural-language
sentence (or short passage) and a candidate FOL formalization produced by any system. There is no gold
formula, no ontology or controlled vocabulary, and no domain knowledge. The metric returns a score
that predicts whether the formalization is faithful to the text, and ideally which kind of error it
contains (quantifier or scope error, dropped or added condition, negation/polarity error, swapped
arguments, conflated or wrongly split concepts). Metrics may score a single formula or a set (several
samples for one sentence, or the formalizations of all sentences in one document). They must be
task-agnostic — usable for any NL→FOL setting, not tied to a domain, dataset or pipeline — and cheap
enough to run on every output (solver calls and small models are fine; a large-LLM judge per formula
only if shown to earn its cost).

Requirements:
- Keep the target fixed: the metric must predict faithfulness to the text. Detecting synthetic
  perturbations, parse validity, or run-to-run stability are not substitutes for that target.
- Validate against ground truth, never against the metric itself. Use public datasets with gold
  sentence-level FOL (e.g. FOLIO, MALLS, or others you find) to build a meta-evaluation set: real
  candidate formalizations from several different systems, plus controlled perturbations of gold
  formulas with known error types, labelled via prover-checked equivalence to gold. Handle two known
  problems: some gold annotations are wrong, and a candidate can be correct without being equivalent
  to the gold (different vocabulary or granularity). Estimate how often each happens and how it
  biases the labels. These datasets are public, so check whether LLM-based metrics benefit from having
  seen the gold.
- For each metric report: correlation with correctness at item and system level; sensitivity per error
  type; invariance under meaning-preserving rewrites (variable/predicate renaming, reordering,
  equivalent restatements); coverage — unparseable outputs are counted and reported, never silently
  dropped; and cost.
- Compare against baselines: parse/compile rate, round-trip (FOL→NL→compare) similarity,
  LLM-as-judge, sampling self-consistency, and the structural consistency metrics described above. A
  new metric matters only if it adds signal those don't.
- Report how each metric's reliability changes with sentence complexity (length, number of quantifiers,
  nesting depth, number of conditions and exceptions). Long, heavily conditioned sentences matter most
  to me.
- Negative results are welcome: if a family of metrics does not track correctness, show that clearly.

Out of scope: comparing grounded vs ungrounded generation (ontology vocabulary injected into prompts),
anything that depends on a particular ontology, and building a better NL→FOL translator. The
contribution is the measurement, not the translator.

Deliverables: reusable Python functions taking (text, fol) — or a set of such pairs — each with a
precise statement of what it measures; the meta-evaluation dataset with its labels; and the results.

I appended a pilot study of this problem with some metrics I already used but haven't verified. Just for reference.
```

```
{"stdout": "\"\"\"T1 head-on API bar: reusable metric wrappers (text, fol) and the statistics core of analyse_T1.py.\n\nORIENTATION CONVENTION (every score returned or consumed here): higher = more likely UNFAITHFUL to the text.\n\nMetric wrappers (async; each takes a budget.Budget so every call is cost-capped and cached in results/llm_cache.jsonl)\n  judge_api(b, text, fol, model, view, rubric='A')   1 - P(faithful) from a rubric-A JSON LLM judge (T=0, max_tokens 150)\n  judge_pyes(b, text, fol, model, view)              1 - P(YES) from next-token logprobs of a one-word YES/NO judge\n  frontier_judge(b, text, fol, view, model=...)      judge_api with a reasoning model (effort low, max_tokens 4000)\n  sc5_api(b, text, k=5)                              k T=0.7 formalisations of the sentence (dataset-E few-shot prompt)\n  sc5_score(candidate, samples)                      (1 - share of samples equivalent to the candidate modulo vocabulary,\n                                                      entropy of the equivalence classes of {candidate} + samples)  [z3]\n  roundtrip_api(b, text, fol)                        FOL -> English (flash-lite VERBALISE) -> the text; NLI / cosine are\n                                                      computed on the GPU by method.py --stage nli --which api\n  view = 'orig' (the sentence and formula as given) or 'disg' (exp-D nonce disguise of content words, both sides).\n\nStatistics core (numpy; sentence-clustered)\n  auroc, tie_rate, strat_auroc, ClusterBoot, paired_boot, boot_ci, delong, matched_fa_threshold, recall_at,\n  build_s4 / nested_delta / permutation_null (cross-fitted logistic stacks on folds_E), gee_slope, gee_slope_diff,\n  consensus_cost_per_item, ipw_auroc, vex_subset (re-exported from src/vocab_exact.py).\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport math\nfrom collections import defaultdict\nfrom typing import Callable, Sequence\n\nimport numpy as np\nfrom scipy.stats import rankdata\n\nSTRATA = (\"L25\", \"L20\", \"EXC\", \"CTRL\")\n\n\n# ===================================================================================================== metric wrappers\nasync def judge_api(b, text: str, fol: str, model: str = \"google/gemini-2.5-flash-lite\", view: str = \"disg\",\n                    rubric: str = \"A\", item_id: str = \"\") -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Rubric-A JSON judge. MEASURES: the model's stated probability that `fol` expresses the meaning of `text`\n    (quantifiers, condition vs assertion, negation, argument order, missing/added content; renaming and logically\n    equivalent rewrites allowed). RETURNS {'score': 1 - P(faithful) or None, 'type', 'fail', 'cost'}.\n    `view` is only recorded: pass the disguised pair (text_d, fol_d) for view='disg'.\"\"\"\n    from . import judges as J\n    rub = J.RUBRIC_A if rubric == \"A\" else J.RUBRIC_B\n    o = await J.judge_json(b, component=\"judge_cheap\", model=model, rubric=rub, text=text, fol=fol, item_id=item_id or view,\n                           user_tmpl=J.USER_JSON_A)\n    return {\"score\": None if o.get(\"p\") is None else 1.0 - o[\"p\"], \"type\": o.get(\"type\"), \"fail\": o.get(\"fail\"),\n            \"cost\": o.get(\"cost\"), \"view\": view}\n\n\nasync def judge_pyes(b, text: str, fol: str, model: str = \"openai/gpt-4.1-nano\", view: str = \"disg\", item_id: str = \"\") -> dict:\n    \"\"\"One-token YES/NO judge (rubric A system prompt). MEASURES: P(YES) renormalised over the YES/NO top-5 logprobs\n    (verdict-token fallback gives p in {0,1}). RETURNS {'score': 1 - P(YES) or None, 'src', 'fail', 'cost'}.\"\"\"\n    from . import judges as J\n    o = await J.judge_logprob(b, component=\"judge_cheap2\", model=model, rubric=J.RUBRIC_A, text=text, fol=fol,\n                              item_id=item_id or view)\n    return {\"score\": None if o.get(\"p\") is None else 1.0 - o[\"p\"], \"src\": o.get(\"src\"), \"fail\": o.get(\"fail\"),\n            \"cost\": o.get(\"cost\"), \"view\": view}\n\n\nasync def frontier_judge(b, text: str, fol: str, view: str = \"orig\", model: str = \"google/gemini-3.1-pro-preview\",\n                         max_tokens: int = 4000, item_id: str = \"\") -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Frontier reasoning judge, same rubric-A JSON prompt (reasoning effort 'low'). Same output as judge_api.\"\"\"\n    from . import judges as J\n    o = await J.judge_json(b, component=\"strong\", model=model, rubric=J.RUBRIC_A, text=text, fol=fol, item_id=item_id or view,\n                           max_tokens=max_tokens, extra={\"reasoning\": {\"effort\": \"low\"}}, user_tmpl=J.USER_JSON_A)\n    return {\"score\": None if o.get(\"p\") is None else 1.0 - o[\"p\"], \"type\": o.get(\"type\"), \"fail\": o.get(\"fail\"),\n            \"cost\": o.get(\"cost\"), \"view\": view}\n\n\nasync def sc5_api(b, text: str, k: int = 5, model: str = \"openai/gpt-4.1-nano\") -> list[str | None]:\n    \"\"\"k independent T=0.7 formalisations of `text` with dataset E's few-shot generation prompt (the samples a\n    self-consistency metric compares the candidate against). RETURNS k extracted formulas (None = failed call).\"\"\"\n    import method as M  # noqa: PLC0415 - the SC call (and its cache key) lives in the orchestrator\n    from . import judges as J\n    outs = [await M._sc_call(b, J, text, \"api_bar\", i) for i in range(k)]\n    forms = [J.extract_formula(o.get(\"text\")) if o.get(\"text\") else None for o in outs]\n    return [f[4:].strip() if f and f.upper().startswith(\"FOL:\") else f for f in forms]\n\n\ndef sc5_score(candidate: str, samples: Sequence[str | None], ms: int = 2000) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"SC-5 modulo vocabulary. MEASURES: disagreement of the candidate with the model's own resamples of the sentence.\n    sc5_eq_frac (oriented) = 1 - #samples equivalent to the candidate (z3, after predicate/constant alignment) / k;\n    sc5_entropy = entropy of the equivalence classes of {candidate} + samples (unparseable samples = singletons).\"\"\"\n    from .consensus_min import sc_scores_one\n    from .labeller.labeller import equivalent_modulo_vocab, parse_ok\n    smp = [s if (s and parse_ok(s)) else None for s in samples]\n    memo: dict = {}\n\n    def eq(a, c):\n        if a == c:\n            return True\n        key = (a, c) if a < c else (c, a)\n        if key not in memo:\n            try:\n                memo[key] = equivalent_modulo_vocab(a, c, ms=ms, do_search=False)[\"cls\"] in (\"EQUIV\", \"VOCAB\", \"GRAN\")\n            except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - a z3 failure counts as not equivalent\n                memo[key] = False\n        return memo[key]\n    r = sc_scores_one(candidate, smp, eq)\n    return {\"sc5_eq_frac\": 1.0 - r[\"sc5_eq_frac\"], \"sc5_entropy\": r[\"sc5_entropy\"]}\n\n\nasync def roundtrip_api(b, text: str, fol: str, model: str = \"google/gemini-2.5-flash-lite\", item_id: str = \"\") -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Round trip step 1: FOL -> one plain English sentence (VERBALISE prompt, T=0). The NLI/cosine comparison with\n    `text` (rt_nli_min = 1 - min(P_entail(text=>v), P_entail(v=>text)); rt_embed_cos = 1 - mpnet cosine) runs on the GPU\n    in method.py --stage nli. RETURNS {'verbalisation', 'leak' (logic symbols left in the output), 'fail', 'cost'}.\"\"\"\n    from . import judges as J\n    g = await J.simple_text(b, component=\"roundtrip\", model=model, prompt=J.VERBALISE.format(fol=fol), item_id=item_id,\n                            max_tokens=120)\n    v, leak = J.clean_verbalisation(g[\"text\"]) if g.get(\"text\") else (None, False)\n    return {\"verbalisation\": v, \"leak\": leak, \"fail\": g.get(\"fail\"), \"cost\": g.get(\"cost\")}\n\n\n# ===================================================================================================== statistics\ndef auroc(y, s) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Mann-Whitney AUROC, ties counted 1/2 (== sklearn roc_auc_score). nan if one class is empty.\"\"\"\n    y = np.asarray(y)\n    s = np.asarray(s, float)\n    n1 = int(y.sum())\n    n0 = len(y) - n1\n    if n1 == 0 or n0 == 0:\n        return float(\"nan\")\n    r = rankdata(s)\n    return float((r[y == 1].sum() - n1 * (n1 + 1) / 2) / (n1 * n0))\n\n\ndef tie_rate(y, s) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Share of (ERROR, CORRECT) pairs whose scores are exactly tied.\"\"\"\n    y = np.asarray(y)\n    s = np.asarray(s, float)\n    pos, neg = s[y == 1], s[y == 0]\n    if len(pos) == 0 or len(neg) == 0:\n        return float(\"nan\")\n    vals, inv = np.unique(np.concatenate([pos, neg]), return_inverse=True)\n    cp = np.bincount(inv[:len(pos)], minlength=len(vals))\n    cn = np.bincount(inv[len(pos):], minlength=len(vals))\n    return float((cp * cn).sum() / (len(pos) * len(neg)))\n\n\ndef strat_auroc(y, s, stratum) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Within-stratum AUROC: sum_h AUC_h * P_h * N_h / sum_h P_h * N_h (only (ERROR, CORRECT) pairs from the same\n    source stratum count), identical to exp 5's strat_auc. Removes the stratum-composition part of pooled AUROC.\"\"\"\n    y = np.asarray(y)\n    s = np.asarray(s, float)\n    stratum = np.asarray(stratum)\n    num = den = 0.0\n    for h in np.unique(stratum):\n        m = stratum == h\n        n1 = int(y[m].sum())\n        n0 = int(m.sum()) - n1\n        if n1 and n0:\n            num += auroc(y[m], s[m]) * n1 * n0\n            den += n1 * n0\n    return num / den if den else float(\"nan\")\n\n\nclass ClusterBoot:\n    \"\"\"Sentence-clustered bootstrap index sets: resample sentence ids with replacement (fixed seed); every metric on a\n    row set uses the IDENTICAL resamples, which makes deltas paired.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, clusters: Sequence[str], b: int = 2000, seed: int = 0):\n        by = defaultdict(list)\n        for i, c in enumerate(clusters):\n            by[c].append(i)\n        keys = sorted(by)\n        arr = [np.array(by[k]) for k in keys]\n        rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n        self.idx = []\n        for _ in range(b):\n            pick = rng.integers(0, len(keys), len(keys))\n            self.idx.append(np.concatenate([arr[j] for j in pick]))\n        self.n_clusters = len(keys)\n\n\ndef boot_ci(vals, lo: float = 2.5, hi: float = 97.5) -> list:\n    v = np.asarray([x for x in vals if x is not None and not (isinstance(x, float) and math.isnan(x))], float)\n    if len(v) == 0:\n        return [None, None]\n    return [float(np.percentile(v, lo)), float(np.percentile(v, hi))]\n\n\ndef paired_boot(y, a, c, cb: ClusterBoot, stat: str = \"pooled\", stratum=None) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Paired delta stat(a) - stat(c) on identical rows; percentile CI over cluster resamples; one-sided\n    p = share of resampled deltas <= 0. stat in {'pooled', 'strat'} (strat needs `stratum`).\"\"\"\n    y = np.asarray(y)\n    a = np.asarray(a, float)\n    c = np.asarray(c, float)\n    st = None if stratum is None else np.asarray(stratum)\n    f = (lambda yy, ss, hh: auroc(yy, ss)) if stat == \"pooled\" else strat_auroc\n    pa, pc = f(y, a, st), f(y, c, st)\n    da, dc, dd = [], [], []\n    for ix in cb.idx:\n        hh = None if st is None else st[ix]\n        xa, xc = f(y[ix], a[ix], hh), f(y[ix], c[ix], hh)\n        da.append(xa)\n        dc.append(xc)\n        dd.append(xa - xc)\n    dd_ = np.array([d for d in dd if not math.isnan(d)])\n    return {\"a\": pa, \"b\": pc, \"a_ci\": boot_ci(da), \"b_ci\": boot_ci(dc), \"delta\": pa - pc, \"ci\": boot_ci(dd),\n            \"p_one_sided_le0\": float(np.mean(dd_ <= 0)) if len(dd_) else None, \"se\": float(np.std(dd_)) if len(dd_) else None,\n            \"n\": int(len(y)), \"n_error\": int(y.sum()), \"n_correct\": int(len(y) - y.sum()), \"stat\": stat}\n\n\ndef boot_stat(y, s, cb: ClusterBoot, stat: str = \"pooled\", stratum=None) -> dict:\n    y = np.asarray(y)\n    s = np.asarray(s, float)\n    st = None if stratum is None else np.asarray(stratum)\n    f = (lambda yy, ss, hh: auroc(yy, ss)) if stat == \"pooled\" else strat_auroc\n    pt = f(y, s, st)\n    bs = [f(y[ix], s[ix], None if st is None else st[ix]) for ix in cb.idx]\n    return {\"auroc\": pt, \"ci\": boot_ci(bs)}\n\n\ndef delong(y, s1, s2) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"DeLong paired test on pooled AUROC (secondary: ignores sentence clustering).\"\"\"\n    from scipy.stats import norm\n    y = np.asarray(y)\n    s1 = np.asarray(s1, float)\n    s2 = np.asarray(s2, float)\n    pos, neg = s1[y == 1], s1[y == 0]\n    pos2, neg2 = s2[y == 1], s2[y == 0]\n    m, n = len(pos), len(neg)\n    if m < 2 or n < 2:\n        return {\"z\": None, \"p\": None}\n\n    def comps(p, q):\n        # V10_i = mean_j [p_i > q_j] + .5[p_i == q_j]; vectorised by ranks\n        allv = np.concatenate([p, q])\n        r_all = rankdata(allv)\n        r_p = rankdata(p)\n        r_q = rankdata(q)\n        v10 = (r_all[:len(p)] - r_p) / len(q)\n        v01 = 1.0 - (r_all[len(p):] - r_q) / len(p)\n        return v10, v01\n    a10, a01 = comps(pos, neg)\n    b10, b01 = comps(pos2, neg2)\n    d = a10.mean() - b10.mean()\n    s10 = np.cov(np.vstack([a10, b10]))\n    s01 = np.cov(np.vstack([a01, b01]))\n    var = (s10[0, 0] + s10[1, 1] - 2 * s10[0, 1]) / m + (s01[0, 0] + s01[1, 1] - 2 * s01[0, 1]) / n\n    if var <= 0:\n        return {\"z\": None, \"p\": None}\n    z = d / math.sqrt(var)\n    return {\"z\": float(z), \"p\": float(2 * (1 - norm.cdf(abs(z))))}\n\n\ndef matched_fa_threshold(neg_vals, fa: float = 0.10) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Threshold t = the (1-fa) quantile of the scores of CORRECT rows (flag = score > t).\"\"\"\n    return float(np.quantile(np.asarray(neg_vals, float), 1 - fa))\n\n\ndef recall_at(y, s, t) -> dict:\n    y = np.asarray(y)\n    s = np.asarray(s, float)\n    f = s > t\n    tp = int((f & (y == 1)).sum())\n    fp = int((f & (y == 0)).sum())\n    return {\"threshold\": float(t), \"recall\": tp / max(1, int(y.sum())), \"fa\": fp / max(1, int((y == 0).sum())),\n            \"precision\": tp / max(1, tp + fp), \"n_flag\": int(f.sum())}\n\n\ndef ipw_auroc(y, s, w) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Weighted Mann-Whitney AUROC: each (ERROR i, CORRECT j) pair weighted w_i * w_j (w = 1 / inclusion prob).\"\"\"\n    y = np.asarray(y)\n    s = np.asarray(s, float)\n    w = np.asarray(w, float)\n    p, n = s[y == 1], s[y == 0]\n    wp, wn = w[y == 1], w[y == 0]\n    if len(p) == 0 or len(n) == 0:\n        return float(\"nan\")\n    gt = (p[:, None] > n[None, :]) + 0.5 * (p[:, None] == n[None, :])\n    ww = wp[:, None] * wn[None, :]\n    return float((gt * ww).sum() / ww.sum())\n\n\n# ===================================================================================================== stacks\ndef build_s4(table: list[dict], labels: dict, folds: dict, feats: list[str], fail_fill: dict, C: float = 1.0):\n    \"\"\"Cross-fitted L2 logistic stack (exp 6 src/s4.fit_s4_oof: standardisation on training folds, missing-indicator\n    columns, FAIL -> worst value). RETURNS (oof {row_key: p}, info).\"\"\"\n    from .s4 import fit_s4_oof\n    return fit_s4_oof(table, labels, folds, feats, C=C, fail_fill=fail_fill)\n\n\ndef nested_delta(y, s_big, s_small, cb: ClusterBoot, stratum=None) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"OOF(S+X) - OOF(S) on identical rows; pooled and stratified, paired cluster bootstrap over OOF predictions\n    (no refit inside the bootstrap: exp D / exp 6 convention; slightly understates variance).\"\"\"\n    return {\"pooled\": paired_boot(y, s_big, s_small, cb, \"pooled\"),\n            \"strat\": paired_boot(y, s_big, s_small, cb, \"strat\", stratum) if stratum is not None else None}\n\n\ndef permutation_null(fit_pair: Callable[[dict], tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]], keys: list[str], y: np.ndarray,\n                     groups: list[str], stratum, n_rep: int = 100, seed: int = 0) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Empirical null of a nested gain: permute labels WITHIN fold x stratum, refit both stacks (fit_pair(perm_labels)\n    -> (oof_big, oof_small) aligned with keys) and record the delta against the permuted labels.\"\"\"\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n    cells = defaultdict(list)\n    for i, g in enumerate(groups):\n        cells[g].append(i)\n    out_p, out_s = [], []\n    st = np.asarray(stratum)\n    for _ in range(n_rep):\n        yy = y.copy()\n        for ix in cells.values():\n            ix = np.array(ix)\n            yy[ix] = rng.permutation(yy[ix])\n        big, small = fit_pair(dict(zip(keys, yy.tolist())))\n        out_p.append(auroc(yy, big) - auroc(yy, small))\n        out_s.append(strat_auroc(yy, big, st) - strat_auroc(yy, small, st))\n    return {\"pooled\": out_p, \"strat\": out_s}\n\n\n# ===================================================================================================== GEE\ndef gee_slope(correct, z, stratum, groups, alt_glm: bool = True) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"GEE Binomial(logit), exchangeable, clustered by sentence: correct ~ z + C(stratum). Returns slope of z.\"\"\"\n    import pandas as pd\n    import statsmodels.api as sm\n    import statsmodels.formula.api as smf\n    df = pd.DataFrame({\"c\": np.asarray(correct, float), \"z\": np.asarray(z, float), \"h\": np.asarray(stratum), \"g\": np.asarray(groups)})\n    try:\n        m = smf.gee(\"c ~ z + C(h)\", groups=\"g\", data=df, family=sm.families.Binomial(),\n                    cov_struct=sm.cov_struct.Exchangeable()).fit()\n        ci_ = m.conf_int().loc[\"z\"].tolist()\n        return {\"slope\": float(m.params[\"z\"]), \"ci_model\": [float(ci_[0]), float(ci_[1])], \"p\": float(m.pvalues[\"z\"]),\n                \"fit\": \"GEE_exchangeable\"}\n    except Exception as e:  # noqa: BLE001 - non-convergence -> cluster-robust GLM (pre-declared fallback)\n        if not alt_glm:\n            raise\n        m = smf.glm(\"c ~ z + C(h)\", data=df, family=sm.families.Binomial()).fit(cov_type=\"cluster\",\n                                                                               cov_kwds={\"groups\": pd.factorize(df[\"g\"])[0]})\n        ci_ = m.conf_int().loc[\"z\"].tolist()\n        return {\"slope\": float(m.params[\"z\"]), \"ci_model\": [float(ci_[0]), float(ci_[1])], \"p\": float(m.pvalues[\"z\"]),\n                \"fit\": f\"GLM_cluster_fallback ({str(e)[:60]})\"}\n\n\ndef _gee_fast_slope(c, z, H, g) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Slope from a GEE fit (used inside the bootstrap).\"\"\"\n    try:\n        return gee_slope(c, z, H, g)[\"slope\"]\n    except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001\n        return float(\"nan\")\n\n\ndef gee_slope_diff(correct_a, correct_b, z, stratum, groups, B: int = 1000, seed: int = 0, n_jobs: int = 7) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"M3 statistic: slope_a - slope_b (log-odds of a correct flag per SD of z), cluster bootstrap over sentences\n    (resampled sentences get fresh cluster ids so duplicates are separate clusters).\"\"\"\n    from joblib import Parallel, delayed\n    ca, cb_, z, H, g = (np.asarray(x) for x in (correct_a, correct_b, z, stratum, groups))\n    sa, sb = gee_slope(ca, z, H, g), gee_slope(cb_, z, H, g)\n    # fresh cluster ids per draw: the position of the cluster in the draw\n    by = defaultdict(list)\n    for i, s in enumerate(g):\n        by[s].append(i)\n    keys = sorted(by)\n    arr = [np.array(by[k]) for k in keys]\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n    draws = [rng.integers(0, len(keys), len(keys)) for _ in range(B)]\n\n    def rep(pick):\n        ix = np.concatenate([arr[j] for j in pick])\n        gid = np.concatenate([np.full(len(arr[j]), n) for n, j in enumerate(pick)])\n        return (_gee_fast_slope(ca[ix], z[ix], H[ix], gid), _gee_fast_slope(cb_[ix], z[ix], H[ix], gid))\n    res = Parallel(n_jobs=n_jobs)(delayed(rep)(p) for p in draws)\n    d = [x - y for x, y in res if not (math.isnan(x) or math.isnan(y))]\n    return {\"slope_a\": sa, \"slope_b\": sb, \"diff\": sa[\"slope\"] - sb[\"slope\"], \"ci\": boot_ci(d), \"B_ok\": len(d), \"B\": B,\n            \"slope_a_ci_boot\": boot_ci([x for x, _ in res]), \"slope_b_ci_boot\": boot_ci([y for _, y in res])}\n\n\n# ===================================================================================================== cost\ndef consensus_cost_per_item(peer_cost_sentence: float, n_candidates_sentence: int, z3_seconds: float,\n                            cpu_usd_per_hour: float = 0.05) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Consensus $/item. FULL = the sentence's peer-pool generation spend / candidates of the sentence + z3 CPU time\n    priced at cpu_usd_per_hour (one core); MARGINAL = z3 CPU only (peer outputs already exist).\"\"\"\n    cpu = z3_seconds / 3600.0 * cpu_usd_per_hour\n    return {\"full\": peer_cost_sentence / max(1, n_candidates_sentence) + cpu, \"marginal\": cpu}\n\n\ndef vex_subset(*a, **k):\n    from .vocab_exact import vex_rows\n    return vex_rows(*a, **k)", "stderr": "", "interrupted": false, "isImage": false, "noOutputExpected": false}
```

````
title: Peer agreement vs paid AI judges on held-out logic
summary: >-
  T1 head-on test on held-out NL->FOL dataset E (8,507 real candidates, 700 sentences). The frozen peer-agreement consensus
  metric (c_score_align, exp 5) and PEER+TEXT fusion (p_peer_text) are compared with newly scored paid API comparators: gemini-2.5-flash-lite
  rubric-A JSON judge (disguised + original, every parseable row), gpt-4.1-nano P(YES) judge (both views), gemini-3.1-pro-preview
  frontier judge (original view, 284-row frame; disguised view blocked by the pre-registered shared-key reserve rule), API
  round trip (flash-lite verbaliser -> DeBERTa NLI/mpnet) and API SC-5 (nano, T=0.7, z3 modulo vocabulary); plus S4_full,
  a cross-fitted stack of all 28 baselines. Pre-registered in prereg_T1.json (sha256 c2a6cf84, frozen before any sweep score).
  Main population R_AB E_POOL PRIMARY n=2,686 (1,822 ERROR/864 CORRECT, 292 sentences); sentence-cluster bootstrap B=2000.
  RESULTS: stratified AUROC c_score_align 0.741 vs flash-lite disguised 0.642 (pooled 0.782 vs 0.696). (a) CONFIRMED: strat
  dAUROC +0.099 [0.049,0.146]; long pool +0.116 [0.070,0.163]; R_A L20+EXC +0.299; also vs best cheap judge (nano orig) +0.089
  [0.043,0.134]. (b) CONFIRMED: nested [S4_full+c]-S4_full strat +0.039 [0.021,0.057], permutation-null p95 0.009; c alone
  ~= S4_full (+0.006 [-0.031,0.042]). (d) CONFIRMED: frame ratio vs frontier 0.957 [0.887,1.034], consensus $1.2e-4 vs frontier
  $3.65e-3/item, nested frame gain +0.037 [0.008,0.066]. VEX aligner-confound control CONFIRMED: +0.306 [0.232,0.376]. M3

GITHUB_DATA_URL = "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ai-inventor-papers/ai-invention-eff060-layered-gold-free-checks-for-logic/fork/run_An1uy2kF2SjZ/round-3/experiment-6/demo/mini_demo_data.json"
import json
from pathlib import Path

def load_data():
    try:
        import urllib.request
        with urllib.request.urlopen(GITHUB_DATA_URL) as response:
            return json.loads(response.read().decode())
    except Exception: pass
    local = Path("mini_demo_data.json")
    if local.exists(): return json.loads(local.read_text())
    raise FileNotFoundError("Could not load mini_demo_data.json")
```
````

### [26] HUMAN-USER prompt · 2026-09-25 16:21:49 UTC

```
I work on converting natural-language text into first-order logic (NL→FOL, autoformalization) with
LLM pipelines. Evaluation is the bottleneck. Correct FOL for a sentence is not unique — different
predicate names, different decompositions of the same concept, and logically equivalent rewrites can
all be correct. Gold FOL annotations are rare and expensive. The metrics in use either need a gold
formula (exact match, BLEU, prover-checked equivalence to the gold) or only check that the output
parses. In my own pilot I used cheap structural metrics: whether a set of generated formulas loads
together without conflicts, whether predicate arities stay consistent, how many predicates are used
but never defined, and how similar the predicate sets are across reruns. Those measure stability and
composability, not whether a formula says what the sentence says.

Find new metrics for NL→FOL output quality. Operating situation: you are given a natural-language
sentence (or short passage) and a candidate FOL formalization produced by any system. There is no gold
formula, no ontology or controlled vocabulary, and no domain knowledge. The metric returns a score
that predicts whether the formalization is faithful to the text, and ideally which kind of error it
contains (quantifier or scope error, dropped or added condition, negation/polarity error, swapped
arguments, conflated or wrongly split concepts). Metrics may score a single formula or a set (several
samples for one sentence, or the formalizations of all sentences in one document). They must be
task-agnostic — usable for any NL→FOL setting, not tied to a domain, dataset or pipeline — and cheap
enough to run on every output (solver calls and small models are fine; a large-LLM judge per formula
only if shown to earn its cost).

Requirements:
- Keep the target fixed: the metric must predict faithfulness to the text. Detecting synthetic
  perturbations, parse validity, or run-to-run stability are not substitutes for that target.
- Validate against ground truth, never against the metric itself. Use public datasets with gold
  sentence-level FOL (e.g. FOLIO, MALLS, or others you find) to build a meta-evaluation set: real
  candidate formalizations from several different systems, plus controlled perturbations of gold
  formulas with known error types, labelled via prover-checked equivalence to gold. Handle two known
  problems: some gold annotations are wrong, and a candidate can be correct without being equivalent
  to the gold (different vocabulary or granularity). Estimate how often each happens and how it
  biases the labels. These datasets are public, so check whether LLM-based metrics benefit from having
  seen the gold.
- For each metric report: correlation with correctness at item and system level; sensitivity per error
  type; invariance under meaning-preserving rewrites (variable/predicate renaming, reordering,
  equivalent restatements); coverage — unparseable outputs are counted and reported, never silently
  dropped; and cost.
- Compare against baselines: parse/compile rate, round-trip (FOL→NL→compare) similarity,
  LLM-as-judge, sampling self-consistency, and the structural consistency metrics described above. A
  new metric matters only if it adds signal those don't.
- Report how each metric's reliability changes with sentence complexity (length, number of quantifiers,
  nesting depth, number of conditions and exceptions). Long, heavily conditioned sentences matter most
  to me.
- Negative results are welcome: if a family of metrics does not track correctness, show that clearly.

Out of scope: comparing grounded vs ungrounded generation (ontology vocabulary injected into prompts),
anything that depends on a particular ontology, and building a better NL→FOL translator. The
contribution is the measurement, not the translator.

Deliverables: reusable Python functions taking (text, fol) — or a set of such pairs — each with a
precise statement of what it measures; the meta-evaluation dataset with its labels; and the results.

I appended a pilot study of this problem with some metrics I already used but haven't verified. Just for reference.
```

RETURNS {'score': 1 - P(faithful) or None, 'type', 'fail', 'cost'}.\n    `view` is only recorded: pass the disguised pair (text_d, fol_d) for view='disg'.\"\"\"\n    from . import judges as J\n    rub = J.RUBRIC_A if rubric == \"A\" else J.RUBRIC_B\n    o = await J.judge_json(b, component=\"judge_cheap\", model=model, rubric=rub, text=text, fol=fol, item_id=item_id or view,\n                           user_tmpl=J.USER_JSON_A)\n    return {\"score\": None if o.get(\"p\") is None else 1.0 - o[\"p\"], \"type\": o.get(\"type\"), \"fail\": o.get(\"fail\"),\n            \"cost\": o.get(\"cost\"), \"view\": view}\n\n\nasync def judge_pyes(b, text: str, fol: str, model: str = \"openai/gpt-4.1-nano\", view: str = \"disg\", item_id: str = \"\") -> dict:\n    \"\"\"One-token YES/NO judge (rubric A system prompt). RETURNS k extracted formulas (None = failed call).\"\"\"\n    import method as M  # noqa: PLC0415 - the SC call (and its cache key) lives in the orchestrator\n    from . import judges as J\n    outs = [await M._sc_call(b, J, text, \"api_bar\", i) for i in range(k)]\n    forms = [J.extract_formula(o.get(\"text\")) if o.get(\"text\") else None for o in outs]\n    return [f[4:].strip() if f and f.upper().startswith(\"FOL:\") else f for f in forms]\n\n\ndef sc5_score(candidate: str, samples: Sequence[str | None], ms: int = 2000) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"SC-5 modulo vocabulary. MEASURES: disagreement of the candidate with the model's own resamples of the sentence.\n    sc5_eq_frac (oriented) = 1 - #samples equivalent to the candidate (z3, after predicate/constant alignment) / k;\n    sc5_entropy = entropy of the equivalence classes of {candidate} + samples (unparseable samples = singletons).\"\"\"\n    from .consensus_min import sc_scores_one\n    from .labeller.labeller import equivalent_modulo_vocab, parse_ok\n    smp = [s if (s and parse_ok(s)) else None for s in samples]\n    memo: dict = {}\n\n    def eq(a, c):\n        if a == c:\n            return True\n        key = (a, c) if a < c else (c, a)\n        if key not in memo:\n            try:\n                memo[key] = equivalent_modulo_vocab(a, c, ms=ms, do_search=False)[\"cls\"] in (\"EQUIV\", \"VOCAB\", \"GRAN\")\n            except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - a z3 failure counts as not equivalent\n                memo[key] = False\n        return memo[key]\n    r = sc_scores_one(candidate, smp, eq)\n    return {\"sc5_eq_frac\": 1.0 - r[\"sc5_eq_frac\"], \"sc5_entropy\": r[\"sc5_entropy\"]}\n\n\nasync def roundtrip_api(b, text: str, fol: str, model: str = \"google/gemini-2.5-flash-lite\", item_id: str = \"\") -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Round trip step 1: FOL -> one plain English sentence (VERBALISE prompt, T=0). The NLI/cosine comparison with\n    `text` (rt_nli_min = 1 - min(P_entail(text=>v), P_entail(v=>text)); rt_embed_cos = 1 - mpnet cosine) runs on the GPU\n    in method.py --stage nli. RETURNS {'verbalisation', 'leak' (logic symbols left in the output), 'fail', 'cost'}.\"\"\"\n    from . import judges as J\n    g = await J.simple_text(b, component=\"roundtrip\", model=model, prompt=J.VERBALISE.format(fol=fol), item_id=item_id,\n                            max_tokens=120)\n    v, leak = J.clean_verbalisation(g[\"text\"]) if g.get(\"text\") else (None, False)\n    return {\"verbalisation\": v, \"leak\": leak, \"fail\": g.get(\"fail\"), \"cost\": g.get(\"cost\")}\n\n\n# ===================================================================================================== statistics\ndef auroc(y, s) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Mann-Whitney AUROC, ties counted 1/2 (== sklearn roc_auc_score). nan if one class is empty.\"\"\"\n    y = np.asarray(y)\n    s = np.asarray(s, float)\n    n1 = int(y.sum())\n    n0 = len(y) - n1\n    if n1 == 0 or n0 == 0:\n        return float(\"nan\")\n    r = rankdata(s)\n    return float((r[y == 1].sum() - n1 * (n1 + 1) / 2) / (n1 * n0))\n\n\ndef tie_rate(y, s) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Share of (ERROR, CORRECT) pairs whose scores are exactly tied.\"\"\"\n    y = np.asarray(y)\n    s = np.asarray(s, float)\n    pos, neg = s[y == 1], s[y == 0]\n    if len(pos) == 0 or len(neg) == 0:\n        return float(\"nan\")\n    vals, inv = np.unique(np.concatenate([pos, neg]), return_inverse=True)\n    cp = np.bincount(inv[:len(pos)], minlength=len(vals))\n    cn = np.bincount(inv[len(pos):], minlength=len(vals))\n    return float((cp * cn).sum() / (len(pos) * len(neg)))\n\n\ndef strat_auroc(y, s, stratum) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Within-stratum AUROC: sum_h AUC_h * P_h * N_h / sum_h P_h * N_h (only (ERROR, CORRECT) pairs from the same\n    source stratum count), identical to exp 5's strat_auc. Removes the stratum-composition part of pooled AUROC.\"\"\"\n    y = np.asarray(y)\n    s = np.asarray(s, float)\n    stratum = np.asarray(stratum)\n    num = den = 0.0\n    for h in np.unique(stratum):\n        m = stratum == h\n        n1 = int(y[m].sum())\n        n0 = int(m.sum()) - n1\n        if n1 and n0:\n            num += auroc(y[m], s[m]) * n1 * n0\n            den += n1 * n0\n    return num / den if den else float(\"nan\")\n\n\nclass ClusterBoot:\n    \"\"\"Sentence-clustered bootstrap index sets: resample sentence ids with replacement (fixed seed); every metric on a\n    row set uses the IDENTICAL resamples, which makes deltas paired.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, clusters: Sequence[str], b: int = 2000, seed: int = 0):\n        by = defaultdict(list)\n        for i, c in enumerate(clusters):\n            by[c].append(i)\n        keys = sorted(by)\n        arr = [np.array(by[k]) for k in keys]\n        rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n        self.idx = []\n        for _ in range(b):\n            pick = rng.integers(0, len(keys), len(keys))\n            self.idx.append(np.concatenate([arr[j] for j in pick]))\n        self.n_clusters = len(keys)\n\n\ndef boot_ci(vals, lo: float = 2.5, hi: float = 97.5) -> list:\n    v = np.asarray([x for x in vals if x is not None and not (isinstance(x, float) and math.isnan(x))], float)\n    if len(v) == 0:\n        return [None, None]\n    return [float(np.percentile(v, lo)), float(np.percentile(v, hi))]\n\n\ndef paired_boot(y, a, c, cb: ClusterBoot, stat: str = \"pooled\", stratum=None) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Paired delta stat(a) - stat(c) on identical rows; percentile CI over cluster resamples; one-sided\n    p = share of resampled deltas <= 0. stat in {'pooled', 'strat'} (strat needs `stratum`).\"\"\"\n    y = np.asarray(y)\n    a = np.asarray(a, float)\n    c = np.asarray(c, float)\n    st = None if stratum is None else np.asarray(stratum)\n    f = (lambda yy, ss, hh: auroc(yy, ss)) if stat == \"pooled\" else strat_auroc\n    pa, pc = f(y, a, st), f(y, c, st)\n    da, dc, dd = [], [], []\n    for ix in cb.idx:\n        hh = None if st is None else st[ix]\n        xa, xc = f(y[ix], a[ix], hh), f(y[ix], c[ix], hh)\n        da.append(xa)\n        dc.append(xc)\n        dd.append(xa - xc)\n    dd_ = np.array([d for d in dd if not math.isnan(d)])\n    return {\"a\": pa, \"b\": pc, \"a_ci\": boot_ci(da), \"b_ci\": boot_ci(dc), \"delta\": pa - pc, \"ci\": boot_ci(dd),\n            \"p_one_sided_le0\": float(np.mean(dd_ <= 0)) if len(dd_) else None, \"se\": float(np.std(dd_)) if len(dd_) else None,\n            \"n\": int(len(y)), \"n_error\": int(y.sum()), \"n_correct\": int(len(y) - y.sum()), \"stat\": stat}\n\n\ndef boot_stat(y, s, cb: ClusterBoot, stat: str = \"pooled\", stratum=None) -> dict:\n    y = np.asarray(y)\n    s = np.asarray(s, float)\n    st = None if stratum is None else np.asarray(stratum)\n    f = (lambda yy, ss, hh: auroc(yy, ss)) if stat == \"pooled\" else strat_auroc\n    pt = f(y, s, st)\n    bs = [f(y[ix], s[ix], None if st is None else st[ix]) for ix in cb.idx]\n    return {\"auroc\": pt, \"ci\": boot_ci(bs)}\n\n\ndef delong(y, s1, s2) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"DeLong paired test on pooled AUROC (secondary: ignores sentence clustering).\"\"\"\n    from scipy.stats import norm\n    y = np.asarray(y)\n    s1 = np.asarray(s1, float)\n    s2 = np.asarray(s2, float)\n    pos, neg = s1[y == 1], s1[y == 0]\n    pos2, neg2 = s2[y == 1], s2[y == 0]\n    m, n = len(pos), len(neg)\n    if m < 2 or n < 2:\n        return {\"z\": None, \"p\": None}\n\n    def comps(p, q):\n        # V10_i = mean_j [p_i > q_j] + .5[p_i == q_j]; vectorised by ranks\n        allv = np.concatenate([p, q])\n        r_all = rankdata(allv)\n        r_p = rankdata(p)\n        r_q = rankdata(q)\n        v10 = (r_all[:len(p)] - r_p) / len(q)\n        v01 = 1.0 - (r_all[len(p):] - r_q) / len(p)\n        return v10, v01\n    a10, a01 = comps(pos, neg)\n    b10, b01 = comps(pos2, neg2)\n    d = a10.mean() - b10.mean()\n    s10 = np.cov(np.vstack([a10, b10]))\n    s01 = np.cov(np.vstack([a01, b01]))\n    var = (s10[0, 0] + s10[1, 1] - 2 * s10[0, 1]) / m + (s01[0, 0] + s01[1, 1] - 2 * s01[0, 1]) / n\n    if var <= 0:\n        return {\"z\": None, \"p\": None}\n    z = d / math.sqrt(var)\n    return {\"z\": float(z), \"p\": float(2 * (1 - norm.cdf(abs(z))))}\n\n\ndef matched_fa_threshold(neg_vals, fa: float = 0.10) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Threshold t = the (1-fa) quantile of the scores of CORRECT rows (flag = score > t).\"\"\"\n    return float(np.quantile(np.asarray(neg_vals, float), 1 - fa))\n\n\ndef recall_at(y, s, t) -> dict:\n    y = np.asarray(y)\n    s = np.asarray(s, float)\n    f = s > t\n    tp = int((f & (y == 1)).sum())\n    fp = int((f & (y == 0)).sum())\n    return {\"threshold\": float(t), \"recall\": tp / max(1, int(y.sum())), \"fa\": fp / max(1, int((y == 0).sum())),\n            \"precision\": tp / max(1, tp + fp), \"n_flag\": int(f.sum())}\n\n\ndef ipw_auroc(y, s, w) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Weighted Mann-Whitney AUROC: each (ERROR i, CORRECT j) pair weighted w_i * w_j (w = 1 / inclusion prob).\"\"\"\n    y = np.asarray(y)\n    s = np.asarray(s, float)\n    w = np.asarray(w, float)\n    p, n = s[y == 1], s[y == 0]\n    wp, wn = w[y == 1], w[y == 0]\n    if len(p) == 0 or len(n) == 0:\n        return float(\"nan\")\n    gt = (p[:, None] > n[None, :]) + 0.5 * (p[:, None] == n[None, :])\n    ww = wp[:, None] * wn[None, :]\n    return float((gt * ww).sum() / ww.sum())\n\n\n# ===================================================================================================== stacks\ndef build_s4(table: list[dict], labels: dict, folds: dict, feats: list[str], fail_fill: dict, C: float = 1.0):\n    \"\"\"Cross-fitted L2 logistic stack (exp 6 src/s4.fit_s4_oof: standardisation on training folds, missing-indicator\n    columns, FAIL -> worst value). RETURNS (oof {row_key: p}, info).\"\"\"\n    from .s4 import fit_s4_oof\n    return fit_s4_oof(table, labels, folds, feats, C=C, fail_fill=fail_fill)\n\n\ndef nested_delta(y, s_big, s_small, cb: ClusterBoot, stratum=None) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"OOF(S+X) - OOF(S) on identical rows; pooled and stratified, paired cluster bootstrap over OOF predictions\n    (no refit inside the bootstrap: exp D / exp 6 convention; slightly understates variance).\"\"\"\n    return {\"pooled\": paired_boot(y, s_big, s_small, cb, \"pooled\"),\n            \"strat\": paired_boot(y, s_big, s_small, cb, \"strat\", stratum) if stratum is not None else None}\n\n\ndef permutation_null(fit_pair: Callable[[dict], tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]], keys: list[str], y: np.ndarray,\n                     groups: list[str], stratum, n_rep: int = 100, seed: int = 0) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Empirical null of a nested gain: permute labels WITHIN fold x stratum, refit both stacks (fit_pair(perm_labels)\n    -> (oof_big, oof_small) aligned with keys) and record the delta against the permuted labels.\"\"\"\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n    cells = defaultdict(list)\n    for i, g in enumerate(groups):\n        cells[g].append(i)\n    out_p, out_s = [], []\n    st = np.asarray(stratum)\n    for _ in range(n_rep):\n        yy = y.copy()\n        for ix in cells.values():\n            ix = np.array(ix)\n            yy[ix] = rng.permutation(yy[ix])\n        big, small = fit_pair(dict(zip(keys, yy.tolist())))\n        out_p.append(auroc(yy, big) - auroc(yy, small))\n        out_s.append(strat_auroc(yy, big, st) - strat_auroc(yy, small, st))\n    return {\"pooled\": out_p, \"strat\": out_s}\n\n\n# ===================================================================================================== GEE\ndef gee_slope(correct, z, stratum, groups, alt_glm: bool = True) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"GEE Binomial(logit), exchangeable, clustered by sentence: correct ~ z + C(stratum). Returns slope of z.\"\"\"\n    import pandas as pd\n    import statsmodels.api as sm\n    import statsmodels.formula.api as smf\n    df = pd.DataFrame({\"c\": np.asarray(correct, float), \"z\": np.asarray(z, float), \"h\": np.asarray(stratum), \"g\": np.asarray(groups)})\n    try:\n        m = smf.gee(\"c ~ z + C(h)\", groups=\"g\", data=df, family=sm.families.Binomial(),\n                    cov_struct=sm.cov_struct.Exchangeable()).fit()\n        ci_ = m.conf_int().loc[\"z\"].tolist()\n        return {\"slope\": float(m.params[\"z\"]), \"ci_model\": [float(ci_[0]), float(ci_[1])], \"p\": float(m.pvalues[\"z\"]),\n                \"fit\": \"GEE_exchangeable\"}\n    except Exception as e:  # noqa: BLE001 - non-convergence -> cluster-robust GLM (pre-declared fallback)\n        if not alt_glm:\n            raise\n        m = smf.glm(\"c ~ z + C(h)\", data=df, family=sm.families.Binomial()).fit(cov_type=\"cluster\",\n                                                                               cov_kwds={\"groups\": pd.factorize(df[\"g\"])[0]})\n        ci_ = m.conf_int().loc[\"z\"].tolist()\n        return {\"slope\": float(m.params[\"z\"]), \"ci_model\": [float(ci_[0]), float(ci_[1])], \"p\": float(m.pvalues[\"z\"]),\n                \"fit\": f\"GLM_cluster_fallback ({str(e)[:60]})\"}\n\n\ndef _gee_fast_slope(c, z, H, g) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Slope from a GEE fit (used inside the bootstrap).\"\"\"\n    try:\n        return gee_slope(c, z, H, g)[\"slope\"]\n    except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001\n        return float(\"nan\")\n\n\ndef gee_slope_diff(correct_a, correct_b, z, stratum, groups, B: int = 1000, seed: int = 0, n_jobs: int = 7) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"M3 statistic: slope_a - slope_b (log-odds of a correct flag per SD of z), cluster bootstrap over sentences\n    (resampled sentences get fresh cluster ids so duplicates are separate clusters).\"\"\"\n    from joblib import Parallel, delayed\n    ca, cb_, z, H, g = (np.asarray(x) for x in (correct_a, correct_b, z, stratum, groups))\n    sa, sb = gee_slope(ca, z, H, g), gee_slope(cb_, z, H, g)\n    # fresh cluster ids per draw: the position of the cluster in the draw\n    by = defaultdict(list)\n    for i, s in enumerate(g):\n        by[s].append(i)\n    keys = sorted(by)\n    arr = [np.array(by[k]) for k in keys]\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n    draws = [rng.integers(0, len(keys), len(keys)) for _ in range(B)]\n\n    def rep(pick):\n        ix = np.concatenate([arr[j] for j in pick])\n        gid = np.concatenate([np.full(len(arr[j]), n) for n, j in enumerate(pick)])\n        return (_gee_fast_slope(ca[ix], z[ix], H[ix], gid), _gee_fast_slope(cb_[ix], z[ix], H[ix], gid))\n    res = Parallel(n_jobs=n_jobs)(delayed(rep)(p) for p in draws)\n    d = [x - y for x, y in res if not (math.isnan(x) or math.isnan(y))]\n    return {\"slope_a\": sa, \"slope_b\": sb, \"diff\": sa[\"slope\"] - sb[\"slope\"], \"ci\": boot_ci(d), \"B_ok\": len(d), \"B\": B,\n            \"slope_a_ci_boot\": boot_ci([x for x, _ in res]), \"slope_b_ci_boot\": boot_ci([y for _, y in res])}\n\n\n# ===================================================================================================== cost\ndef consensus_cost_per_item(peer_cost_sentence: float, n_candidates_sentence: int, z3_seconds: float,\n                            cpu_usd_per_hour: float = 0.05) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Consensus $/item. FULL = the sentence's peer-pool generation spend / candidates of the sentence + z3 CPU time\n    priced at cpu_usd_per_hour (one core); MARGINAL = z3 CPU only (peer outputs already exist).\"\"\"\n    cpu = z3_seconds / 3600.0 * cpu_usd_per_hour\n    return {\"full\": peer_cost_sentence / max(1, n_candidates_sentence) + cpu, \"marginal\": cpu}\n\n\ndef vex_subset(*a, **k):\n    from .vocab_exact import vex_rows\n    return vex_rows(*a, **k)", "stderr": "", "interrupted": false, "isImage": false, "noOutputExpected": false}

```
import json, random, collections
d=json.load(open("$A/full_method_out.json"))
an=json.load(open("$A/results/analysis_T1.json"))
ver=json.load(open("$A/results/verdict_T1.json"))
ex=d["datasets"][0]["examples"]
P=[e for e in ex if e["metadata_in_R_AB"] and e["metadata_pool"]=="E_POOL" and e["metadata_parse_ok"]]
rng=random.Random(0)
chosen=[]; used_keys=set(); sent_count=collections.Counter()
def take(pool, n):
    pool=pool[:]; rng.shuffle(pool); got=0
    for e in pool:
        if got>=n: break
        k=e["metadata_canonical_key"]
        if k in used_keys or sent_count[e["metadata_sentence_id"]]>=2: continue
        chosen.append(e); used_keys.add(k); sent_count[e["metadata_sentence_id"]]+=1; got+=1
# 1) frontier-frame rows (judge_strong_orig scored): 36, balanced by label
fr=[e for e in P if e["predict_judge_strong_orig"]!="NA"]
for lab in ("ERROR","CORRECT"):
    take([e for e in fr if e["output"]==lab], 18)
# 2) per stratum top-up to 16 rows, balanced label; L20/EXC prefer tier-A (R_A) rows
for h in ("L25","L20","EXC","CTRL"):
    for lab in ("ERROR","CORRECT"):
        have=sum(1 for e in chosen if e["metadata_strata"]["source_stratum"]==h and e["output"]==lab)
        need=max(0,8-have)
        pool=[e for e in P if e["metadata_strata"]["source_stratum"]==h and e["output"]==lab]
        if h in ("L20","EXC"):
            take([e for e in pool if e["metadata_in_R_A"]], need)
        else:
            take(pool, need)
# 3) fill to 100 with random P rows
take(P, 100-len(chosen))
chosen=chosen[:100]
print(len(chosen), collections.Counter((e["metadata_strata"]["source_stratum"],e["output"]) for e in chosen))
print("frame", sum(e["predict_judge_strong_orig"]!="NA" for e in chosen), "RA", sum(e["metadata_in_R_A"] for e in chosen),
      "RA L20EXC", collections.Counter(e["output"] for e in chosen if e["metadata_in_R_A"] and e["metadata_strata"]["source_stratum"] in ("L20","EXC")),
      "sentences", len({e["metadata_sentence_id"] for e in chosen}), "systems", len({e["metadata_sysvar"] for e in chosen}))
# keep original order of the full file
order={e["metadata_canonical_key"]:i for i,e in enumerate(ex)}
chosen.sort(key=lambda e: order[e["metadata_canonical_key"]])
out={"metadata":{**d["metadata"],
  "demo_subset":"100 rows of the main population (E_POOL, PRIMARY/parseable, label regime R_AB) of full_method_out.json: 36 frontier-frame rows (gemini-3.1-pro-preview scored, 18 ERROR/18 CORRECT) + >=8 ERROR and >=8 CORRECT rows per source stratum (L25, L20, EXC, CTRL; L20/EXC drawn from tier-A = R_A rows), at most 2 rows per sentence, seed 0.",
  "full_population":an["population"],
  "cost_per_item_usd":an["cost"]["per_item_usd"],
  "full_run_verdict":ver},
 "datasets":[{"dataset":d["datasets"][0]["dataset"],"examples":chosen}]}
json.dump(out, open("$W/mini_demo_data.json","w"), ensure_ascii=False, indent=1)
EOF
ls -la $W/mini_demo_data.json
```

````
File: /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_An1uy2kF2SjZ/4_gen_paper_repo/_4_gen_demo_art/notebook_workspaces/iter_3/art_7GxreYjATkC5/README.md

# Demo: peer agreement vs paid AI judges on held-out NL→FOL (T1)

This is a runnable, Colab-ready demo notebook for the T1 experiment. The experiment asks whether the frozen peer-agreement consensus metric `c_score_align` predicts NL→FOL faithfulness better than cheap paid API LLM judges, and whether it still adds signal beyond a cross-fitted stack of every baseline (`S4_full`).

## What was done

The original artifact is a staged orchestrator (`method.py --stage …`). Its scoring stages need a paid OpenRouter key, GPUs and the 8,507-row dataset E. The notebook re-runs its **analysis stage**, `stage_t1_analysis` → `src/analyse_T1.py` + `src/api_bar.py`, on a curated 100-row subset of the stored per-item scores. The code is copied with minimal changes, split into cells, and each cell has an explanation:
- the sanity toy checks from `method.py::_t0_unit_tests`;
- the statistics core (AUROC, within-stratum AUROC, sentence-cluster bootstrap, paired deltas, DeLong, matched-FA recall);
- the `Table` / `Cell` machinery;
- criterion (a), the head-on test vs every judge;
- criterion (b), the nested gain over `S4_full`, from the stored out-of-fold predictions;
- criterion (d), the frontier-judge ratio and nested frame test;
- the complexity bins and the placebo.

The bootstrap sizes are the original ones (B = 2000, sub-cells 1000, placebo 200). The whole notebook runs in about 1 minute.

The full run confirmed (a), (b) and (d) on n = 2,686. On 100 rows the point estimates have the same sign for (a) and (d), but the CIs are too wide to confirm them. The notebook prints the demo numbers beside the full-run numbers.

**What the demo does not run:**
- API or GPU scoring;
- the 28-feature S4 refit and its permutation null, because the features are not in the demo data;
- the M3 GEE bootstrap;
- the COVERAGE and contested-label cells;
- the IPW frame weights.

## Layout

| path | what |
|---|---|
| `code_demo.ipynb` | The demo notebook, executed, with outputs. It loads data from the GitHub raw URL and falls back to the local file. |
| `mini_demo_data.json` | Curated subset of 100 rows from the main population (E_POOL, parseable, R_AB labels) of `full_method_out.json`. It has 46 frontier-frame rows, and every source stratum (L25/L20/EXC/CTRL) has both labels. No sentence contributes more than 2 rows, and the draw uses seed 0. Each row has the sentence, the candidate FOL, the label, strata metadata and every metric's oriented score (`predict_*`). `metadata` also holds the full-run costs, the frozen thresholds, the full-run AUROCs and the verdict. |
| `README.md` | This file. |
| `.aii/manifest.yaml` | Storage decisions. There are no heavy files. |

## How to run

- **Colab:** open `code_demo.ipynb` and choose Run all. The install cell installs only `loguru` and uses Colab's pre-installed numpy, scipy, scikit-learn, pandas and matplotlib.
- **Locally (Python 3.12):**
  ```bash
  pip install jupyter ipykernel
  jupyter nbconvert --to notebook --execute code_demo.ipynb --output code_demo.ipynb
  ```
  Locally, the install cell pins numpy 2.0.2, pandas 2.2.2, scipy 1.16.3, scikit-learn 1.6.1 and matplotlib 3.10.0, which are Colab's versions.

The tunable parameters (`B`, `B_SMALL`, `N_PLACEBO`) are in the configuration cell and are already set to the original values.

## Restoring removed files

Nothing is marked for deletion: every file here is small and is kept. You can recreate the Python environment at any time by running the notebook's first cell.
````

### [90] ASSISTANT · 2026-09-25 16:30:03 UTC

```
I've built the demo notebook and its 100-row data file. The notebook runs cleanly in the fresh test environment, with no cell errors, in about 60 seconds with the original settings (limit: 600 s).

**What the notebook runs.** `method.py`'s scoring steps need a paid OpenRouter key, GPUs and the 8,507-row dataset, so they can't run in Colab. The notebook instead re-runs the final analysis step on the scores the full run already stored. That code is copied from `src/api_bar.py` and `src/analyse_T1.py`, which `method.py` calls, and split into explained cells. It covers:
* the small self-checks from `method.py`;
* the statistics (AUROC, within-stratum AUROC, sentence-level bootstrap, paired differences, DeLong test);
* criterion (a), consensus vs each judge;
* criterion (b), consensus added on top of the stack of all baselines;
* criterion (d), consensus vs the frontier judge;
* reliability by sentence complexity, and a shuffled-label check;
* a summary table and a three-panel figure.

The only new code is a small adapter that rebuilds the original row and score tables from the demo data.

**Results on 100 rows.** None of the three criteria can be confirmed at this size, though (a) and (d) point the same way as the full run. The confidence intervals are about three times wider than in the full run (2,686 rows), which confirmed all three. The notebook prints both side by side and labels the demo result "not shown at n=100" rather than "disconfirmed".

| | Demo (100 rows) | Full run |
|---|---|---|
| (a) consensus − flash-lite judge | +0.049 [−0.12, 0.22] | +0.099 [0.049, 0.146] |
| (b) gain over the baseline stack | −0.002 [−0.07, 0.07] | +0.039 [0.021, 0.057] |
| (d) ratio to frontier judge | 0.885 | 0.957 |

**Parts of the original that don't run:**
* **Stack refit and permutation test for (b):** the 28 input features aren't in the demo data, so (b) uses the stored out-of-fold predictions.
* **Length-slope comparison (M3):** its long statistical fits are skipped.
* **Frame weighting for (d):** the inclusion probabilities aren't available, so the weighted AUROC is skipped.
* **Two label groups:** unparseable and contested rows aren't in the subset.

**The data file.** Every row comes from the main population, with its scores and metadata:
* 46 rows scored by the frontier judge;
* both labels in each of the four sentence groups;
* at most 2 rows per sentence (89 sentences);
* randomly drawn with seed 0.

Its metadata also carries the full run's costs, thresholds, AUROCs and verdict for comparison.

**The URL and data loading.** The GitHub data URL is in the notebook exactly as given. Loading tries that URL first and falls back to the local file.

I also wrote a README and an empty storage manifest, since nothing in the workspace is large, plus the structured output file. There was no to-do tool in this session, so I followed the steps in order without one. I generated the notebook with a small builder script (kept outside the workspace) rather than the notebook-edit tool.

Files are in the workspace:
- `code_demo.ipynb`
- `mini_demo_data.json`
- `README.md`
- `.aii/manifest.yaml`
- `.terminal_claude_agent_struct_out.json`
```
